# Power Windows System Description - Control/Function (5-door) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

Basic Control/Function

- Manual Up/Down Function The 1-step operation of the power window switch moves the window up or down. This is available when the vehicle is in the ON mode or OFF mode (For about 10 minutes after the vehicle is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode).

The 1-step operation of the power window switch moves the window up or down. This is available when the vehicle is in the ON mode or OFF mode (For about 10 minutes after the vehicle is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode).

- Auto Up/Down Function The 2-step operation of the power window switch moves the window up or down. This is available when the vehicle is in the ON mode or OFF mode (For about 10 minutes after the vehicle is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode).

The 2-step operation of the power window switch moves the window up or down. This is available when the vehicle is in the ON mode or OFF mode (For about 10 minutes after the vehicle is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode).

Auto Reverse Power Window Function

When an object is detected during the auto up operation, the window stops closing and reverses about 70 mm (2.76 in). If the window is less than halfway opened (about 220 mm (8.66 in)), it will reverse to the half open position (about 220 mm (8.66 in)). In manual operation, this function is active only during the vehicle OFF operation. This function is activated based on the calculation of the rotation speed and load change of the motor's pulse A.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Vehicle OFF Operation

For about 10 minutes after the vehicle is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode, the window's open/close operation with the power window switches is available.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Vehicle OFF Operation Start Condition | Vehicle ON mode -> OFF (LOCK) mode Driver's door switch OFF (Door close) Front Passenger's door switch OFF (Door close)

- Vehicle ON mode -> OFF (LOCK) mode

- Driver's door switch OFF (Door close)

- Front Passenger's door switch OFF (Door close)

Vehicle OFF Operation Cancel Condition | Timer | Vehicle ON mode -> OFF (LOCK) mode and past 10 minutes

Door | Driver's door switch ON (Door open) Front Passenger's door switch ON (Door open)

- Driver's door switch ON (Door open)

- Front Passenger's door switch ON (Door open)

Keyless/Key Cylinder Operation

By turning the key a second time and holding it with the key inserted into the driver's door key cylinder, the windows and moonroof close (or open) (turning the key clockwise to open, turning the key counterclockwise to close). The windows and moonroof stop moving when the key is returned to the neutral position.

By pressing and holding the UNLOCK button of the remote a second time, the windows and moonroof open. The windows and moonroof stop moving when the UNLOCK button is released. The windows do not close with the LOCK button.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Keyless Operation | Open | Possible

Close | Impossible

Key Cylinder Operation | Open | Possible

Close | Possible
````

### Full Text

# Door Position Adjustment: Adjustment

NOTE: Check for a flush fit with the body, then check for equal gaps between the front door and the rear door, and between the doors and the body. Check that the door and body edges are parallel.

- Front Fender Fairing - Remove

- Door Position - Adjust Fig 1: Front Door Position Adjustment Components With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Fig 2: Rear Door Position Adjustment Components With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: Place the vehicle on a firm, level surface when adjusting the doors. 1. Adjust at the hinges (A): Pad the floor jack (B) with the shop towel (C), then use the jack to support the door while adjusting it. Loosen the hinge mounting bolts (D) slightly, and move the door backward or forward, up or down as necessary to equalize the gaps. 2. If necessary, replace the door mounting bolts with the adjusting bolts made specifically for door adjustment, then adjust at the door: Loosen the door mounting bolts (E) slightly, and move the door up or down as necessary to equalize the gaps, and move it in or out until it is flush with the body. NOTE: Refer to the Parts Catalog if you need use an adjusting bolt. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Check that the door and body edges are parallel. If necessary, adjust the door cushions (A) to make the rear of the doors flush with the body. 4. Make sure the door opens without popping or binding and latches securely without slamming. If not, adjust the door striker . 5. Apply touch-up paint to the hinge mounting bolts and around the hinges.

# Door Striker Adjustment: Adjustment

- Door Striker - Adjust Fig 1: Door Striker Adjustment Components With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Make sure the door latches securely without slamming it. If necessary, adjust the striker (A): The striker can be adjusted slightly up or down, and in or out. 1. Loosen the screws (B) with a TORX T40 bit. 2. Wrap the striker with a shop towel, then adjust the striker by tapping it with a plastic hammer (C). Do not tap the striker too hard. 3. Lightly tighten the screws. 4. Hold the outer handle out, and push the door against the body to be sure the striker allows a flush fit. If the door latches properly, tighten the screws to the specified torque, and recheck.

Make sure the door latches securely without slamming it. If necessary, adjust the striker (A): The striker can be adjusted slightly up or down, and in or out. 1. Loosen the screws (B) with a TORX T40 bit. 2. Wrap the striker with a shop towel, then adjust the striker by tapping it with a plastic hammer (C). Do not tap the striker too hard. 3. Lightly tighten the screws. 4. Hold the outer handle out, and push the door against the body to be sure the striker allows a flush fit. If the door latches properly, tighten the screws to the specified torque, and recheck.

1. Loosen the screws (B) with a TORX T40 bit.

2. Wrap the striker with a shop towel, then adjust the striker by tapping it with a plastic hammer (C). Do not tap the striker too hard.

3. Lightly tighten the screws.

4. Hold the outer handle out, and push the door against the body to be sure the striker allows a flush fit. If the door latches properly, tighten the screws to the specified torque, and recheck.
````

# Removal and Installation

- Front Door Sash Outer Trim - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Pull away the front door weatherstrip (A), then remove the screw (B) securing the front door outer molding (C). 2. Pull out the glass run channel (D) as needed. 3. Cut the double-sided adhesive tapes (E) with a utility knife. 4. Remove the upper edge of the front door sash outer trim (F) from the front door outer molding. NOTE: Pull the front door outer molding only as needed to remove the upper edge of the front door sash outer trim. Excessive pulling will cause the molding to get damaged resulting in replacement. 5. Release the hook (G), then remove the front door sash outer trim. 6. Remove any remaining double-sided adhesive tapes from the front door sash outer trim and the front door sash, then clean the surfaces with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the clean surface.

NOTE: Pull the front door outer molding only as needed to remove the upper edge of the front door sash outer trim. Excessive pulling will cause the molding to get damaged resulting in replacement. 5. - All Removed Parts - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal. NOTE: If you are reusing the original front door sash outer trim (A), apply the adhesive primer to the areas where the new double-sided adhesive tapes (B, C) will be applied, then attach the new double-sided adhesive tapes to the front door sash outer trim along the alignment lines (D) as shown. # Removal and Installation

- Rear Door Sash Outer Trim - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Pull away the rear door weatherstrip (A), then remove the screw (B) securing the rear door outer molding (C). 2. Pull out the glass run channel (D) as needed. 3. Cut the double-sided adhesive tapes (E) with a utility knife. 4. Release the upper edge of the rear door sash outer trim (F) from the rear door outer molding. NOTE: Pull the rear door outer molding only as needed to remove the upper edge of the rear door sash outer trim. Excessive pulling will cause the molding to get damaged resulting in replacement. 5. Release the hook (G), then remove the rear door sash outer trim. 6. Remove any remaining double-sided adhesive tapes from the rear door sash outer trim and the rear door sash, then clean the surfaces with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the clean surface.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Pull away the rear door weatherstrip (A), then remove the screw (B) securing the rear door outer molding (C). 2. Pull out the glass run channel (D) as needed. 3. Cut the double-sided adhesive tapes (E) with a utility knife. 4. Release the upper edge of the rear door sash outer trim (F) from the rear door outer molding. NOTE: Pull the rear door outer molding only as needed to remove the upper edge of the rear door sash outer trim. Excessive pulling will cause the molding to get damaged resulting in replacement. 5. Release the hook (G), then remove the rear door sash outer trim. 6. Remove any remaining double-sided adhesive tapes from the rear door sash outer trim and the rear door sash, then clean the surfaces with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the clean surface.

2. Pull out the glass run channel (D) as needed.

3. Cut the double-sided adhesive tapes (E) with a utility knife.

4. Release the upper edge of the rear door sash outer trim (F) from the rear door outer molding.

NOTE: Pull the rear door outer molding only as needed to remove the upper edge of the rear door sash outer trim. Excessive pulling will cause the molding to get damaged resulting in replacement.

5. Release the hook (G), then remove the rear door sash outer trim.

6. Remove any remaining double-sided adhesive tapes from the rear door sash outer trim and the rear door sash, then clean the surfaces with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the clean surface.

- All Removed Parts - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal. NOTE: If you are reusing the original rear door sash outer trim (A), apply the adhesive primer to the areas where the new double-sided adhesive tapes (B, C) will be applied, then attach the new double-sided adhesive tapes to the rear door sash outer trim along the alignment lines (D) as shown. Adhesive tape (B): Thickness 0.8 mm (0.031 in) Width 10 mm (0.39 in) Length 345 mm (13.58 in) (C): Thickness 0.8 mm (0.031 in) Width 15 mm (0.59 in) Length 345 mm (13.58 in)

# Removal and Installation

- Vehicle - Lift

- Front Brace - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Remove the bolts (A). 2. Loosen the bolts (B) until they engage the inside threads of the collar bolts (C). The thread lock on the bolts makes the bolts and the collar bolts turn together. 3. Continue to loosen the bolts to turn the collar bolts into the fixed space adjusters (D). This creates a gap (E) between the collar bolts and the body. 4. Remove the bolts (B), then remove the front brace (F).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Remove the bolts (A). 2. Loosen the bolts (B) until they engage the inside threads of the collar bolts (C). The thread lock on the bolts makes the bolts and the collar bolts turn together. 3. Continue to loosen the bolts to turn the collar bolts into the fixed space adjusters (D). This creates a gap (E) between the collar bolts and the body. 4. Remove the bolts (B), then remove the front brace (F).

2. Loosen the bolts (B) until they engage the inside threads of the collar bolts (C). The thread lock on the bolts makes the bolts and the collar bolts turn together.

3. Continue to loosen the bolts to turn the collar bolts into the fixed space adjusters (D). This creates a gap (E) between the collar bolts and the body.

4. Remove the bolts (B), then remove the front brace (F).

- All Removed Parts - Install Fig 1: Front Brace Components And Bolts With Tightening Sequence And Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal. NOTE: Before installing the front brace (A), be sure the collar bolt (B) can be screwed/unscrewed lightly by hand, and then screw them into the space adjuster (C) fully by hand. Do not tighten them with any tools. Before installing the front brace, screw the special bolt into the collar bolts, and check that they turn together. If they do not turn together, replace the bolts. After setting the front brace in the body, install all of the mounting bolts but do not tighten them. First tighten the bolt (D). Next, tighten the bolt (E). Then tighten the bolts (F). As you tighten the bolts, the collar bolts screw out of the space adjuster until they contact the body. Continue tightening the bolts to the specified torque.

1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal. NOTE: Before installing the front brace (A), be sure the collar bolt (B) can be screwed/unscrewed lightly by hand, and then screw them into the space adjuster (C) fully by hand. Do not tighten them with any tools. Before installing the front brace, screw the special bolt into the collar bolts, and check that they turn together. If they do not turn together, replace the bolts. After setting the front brace in the body, install all of the mounting bolts but do not tighten them. First tighten the bolt (D). Next, tighten the bolt (E). Then tighten the bolts (F). As you tighten the bolts, the collar bolts screw out of the space adjuster until they contact the body. Continue tightening the bolts to the specified torque.

- Before installing the front brace (A), be sure the collar bolt (B) can be screwed/unscrewed lightly by hand, and then screw them into the space adjuster (C) fully by hand. Do not tighten them with any tools.

- Before installing the front brace, screw the special bolt into the collar bolts, and check that they turn together. If they do not turn together, replace the bolts.

- After setting the front brace in the body, install all of the mounting bolts but do not tighten them. First tighten the bolt (D). Next, tighten the bolt (E). Then tighten the bolts (F). As you tighten the bolts, the collar bolts screw out of the space adjuster until they contact the body. Continue tightening the bolts to the specified torque.
````

- Front Subframe - Tightening Torque NOTE: How to read the torque specifications . Align the front subframe with the subframe alignment pin. Align the front subframe in the following sequence. 1. Lift the front subframe (A) up to the body, and loosely install the new subframe mounting bolts (B) and the new front subframe rear stay mounting bolts (C). Fig 1: Front Subframe And Bolts With Tightening Sequence And Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Insert the subframe alignment pin (D) through the positioning hole (E) on the right rear subframe, and into the positioning hole (F) on the body, then loosely tighten the subframe right rear mounting bolt. 3. Insert the subframe alignment pin through the positioning hole on the left rear subframe, and into the positioning hole on the body, then loosely tighten the subframe left rear mounting bolt. 4. Tighten the subframe mounting bolts to the specified torque in the numbered sequence as shown. Use the subframe alignment pin when tightening the rear side subframe mounting bolts. 5. Check all of the subframe mounting bolts, and retighten if necessary. 6. Tighten the front subframe rear stay mounting bolts to the specified torque. 7. After reinstalling all removed parts, check and adjust the front wheel alignment .

NOTE:

- How to read the torque specifications .

- Align the front subframe with the subframe alignment pin.

- Align the front subframe in the following sequence.

1. Lift the front subframe (A) up to the body, and loosely install the new subframe mounting bolts (B) and the new front subframe rear stay mounting bolts (C).

2. Insert the subframe alignment pin (D) through the positioning hole (E) on the right rear subframe, and into the positioning hole (F) on the body, then loosely tighten the subframe right rear mounting bolt.

3. Insert the subframe alignment pin through the positioning hole on the left rear subframe, and into the positioning hole on the body, then loosely tighten the subframe left rear mounting bolt.

4. Tighten the subframe mounting bolts to the specified torque in the numbered sequence as shown. Use the subframe alignment pin when tightening the rear side subframe mounting bolts.

5. Check all of the subframe mounting bolts, and retighten if necessary.

6. Tighten the front subframe rear stay mounting bolts to the specified torque.

7. After reinstalling all removed parts, check and adjust the front wheel alignment

.

Rear Subframe

- Rear Subframe - Tightening Torque NOTE: During installation, align both positioning holes on the rear subframe with the positioning holes on the body using the subframe alignment pin as a guide. First align the right positioning hole, and next align the left positioning hole. 1. Install the rear subframe. Fig 2: Rear Subframe And Bolts With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. After reinstalling all removed parts, check and adjust the rear wheel alignment .

NOTE: During installation, align both positioning holes on the rear subframe with the positioning holes on the body using the subframe alignment pin as a guide. First align the right positioning hole, and next align the left positioning hole.

- Title: Moonroof Glass Roof Seal Removal and Installation: Removal

# Moonroof Glass Roof Seal Removal and Installation: Removal

- Moonroof Glass - Remove

- Glass Roof Seal - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Place a piece of masking tape (A) on the middle of the front edge of the moonroof glass (B). Make sure that the tape is on the glass only, and is not touching the glass roof seal (C). 2. Place a mark (D) on the masking tape that aligns with the seam (E) of the glass roof seal. 3. Push down on the top of the original glass roof seal with your thumb until a small section separates from the moonroof glass. Once a section has started to separate, grip it and pull outward, working around the moonroof glass until the glass roof seal is completely removed (note the locations of the holes in the glass roof seal).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Place a piece of masking tape (A) on the middle of the front edge of the moonroof glass (B). ## Chunk 3610: Moonroof Glass Roof Seal Removal and Installation: Installation

- Title: Moonroof Glass Roof Seal Removal and Installation: Installation
- Source path: `pages\3201.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_1ff67923f376`
- Images: `images\GHH410586.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16123.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Moonroof Glass Roof Seal Removal and Installation: Installation

- Glass Roof Seal - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Align the seam (A) of the new glass roof seal (B) to the mark (C) on the masking tape (D). Make sure the holes are pointed in the same direction as noted in removal procedure. 2. Press the ridge of the new glass roof seal into the groove on the moonroof glass starting at the seam. Work toward one corner; then go back to the middle and work toward the opposite corner. NOTE: Be careful not to stretch the glass roof seal. 3. Continue pressing along one side, from corner to corner; then press along the opposite side, from corner to corner. 4. At the rear of the moonroof glass, continue pressing from one corner to the middle; then press from the opposite corner to the middle until the glass roof seal is completely installed. 5. Check for proper fit of the new glass roof seal by opening and closing the moonroof.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Align the seam (A) of the new glass roof seal (B) to the mark (C) on the masking tape (D). Continue pressing along one side, from corner to corner; then press along the opposite side, from corner to corner. 4. At the rear of the moonroof glass, continue pressing from one corner to the middle; then press from the opposite corner to the middle until the glass roof seal is completely installed. 5. # Moonroof Glass Position Adjustment: Adjustment

- Moonroof Glass Position - Check Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. The roof panel (A) should be even with the glass roof seal (B) to within 0+1/-1 mm (0+0.04/-0.04 in) all the way around. If not, make the following adjustment:

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The roof panel (A) should be even with the glass roof seal (B) to within 0+1/-1 mm (0+0.04/-0.04 in) all the way around. If not, make the following adjustment:

- Moonroof Glass Position - Adjust Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Slightly loosen the bolts (A) with a TORX T25 bit. 2. Move the glass (B) up or down. 3. Tighten all bolts securely. 4. If necessary, repeat on the opposite side.

- Water Leaks - Check

# Moonroof Motor-Control Unit Connector for Inputs and Outputs

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Terminal number | Terminal name | Description | Signal

1 | +B SUNROOF | Power source for moonroof motor-control unit | About battery voltage at all times

2 | Not used | --- | ---

3 | Not used | --- | ---

4 | GND | Ground for moonroof motor-control unit | Less than 0.2 V at all times

5 | Not used | --- | ---

6 | Not used | --- | ---

7 | S/R SW OPEN | Detects S/R SW OPEN signal | With moonroof switch OPEN: less than 0.2 V If none of above conditions are met: about battery voltage

8 | S/R SW TILT | Detects S/R SW TILT signal | With moonroof switch TILT: less than 0.2 V If none of above conditions are met: about battery voltage

9 | S/R SW CLOSE | Detects S/R SW CLOSE signal | With moonroof switch CLOSE: less than 0.2 V If none of above conditions are met: about battery voltage

10 | Not used | --- | ---

11 | Not used | --- | ---

12 | LIN(P/W) | Communication line | ---

13 | Not used | --- | ---

14 | S/R SW AUTO | Detects S/R SW AUTO signal | With moonroof switch AUTO: less than 0.2 V If none of above conditions are met: about battery voltage
````

## Chunk 3623: Resetting the Moonroof Motor-Control Unit: Procedure

- Title: Resetting the Moonroof Motor-Control Unit: Procedure
- Source path: `pages\3216.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_d73f5c225e80`
- Images: `images\GHH410552.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16139.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Resetting the Moonroof Motor-Control Unit: Procedure

- Moonroof Control Unit - Reset Resetting the moonroof is required when any of the following have occurred: The moonroof stops on the way during AUTO/MANUAL operation. The moonroof does not work during auto operation. The moonroof was moved manually while the 12 volt battery was dead or disconnected. The moonroof motor was removed or replaced with a new one. Any of components related to the moonroof were replaced or removed and installed. - Wind deflector - Moonroof glass - Moonroof seal - Moonroof glass bracket - Moonroof frame/drain channel slider and cable assembly - Moonroof motor - Moonroof cables, etc. To reset the moonroof control unit, do these steps: 1. Close the driver's door, and leave it closed until the procedure is complete. 2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Press and hold the tilt switch, and turn the vehicle to the ON mode. 4. Release the tilt switch, and turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. 5. Repeat steps 3 and 4 four more times. 6. Check if the AUTO OPEN and AUTO CLOSE function still work. If they still work, the AUTO functions have not been cleared, go back to step 1. If the AUTO functions have been cleared, go to step 7. 7. Press and hold the moonroof close switch until the moonroof is tilted fully open. 8. Confirm that the moonroof control unit is reset by using the moonroof AUTO OPEN and AUTO CLOSE function.

Resetting the moonroof is required when any of the following have occurred:

- The moonroof stops on the way during AUTO/MANUAL operation.

- The moonroof does not work during auto operation.

- The moonroof was moved manually while the 12 volt battery was dead or disconnected.

- The moonroof motor was removed or replaced with a new one.

- Any of components related to the moonroof were replaced or removed and installed.

- - Wind deflector

- - Wind deflector

Wind deflector

- - Moonroof glass

- - Moonroof glass

Moonroof glass

- - Moonroof seal

- - Moonroof seal

Moonroof seal

- - Moonroof glass bracket

- - Moonroof glass bracket

Moonroof glass bracket

- - Moonroof frame/drain channel slider and cable assembly

- - Moonroof frame/drain channel slider and cable assembly

Moonroof frame/drain channel slider and cable assembly

- - Moonroof motor

- - Moonroof motor

Moonroof motor

- - Moonroof cables, etc.

- - Moonroof cables, etc.

Moonroof cables, etc.

To reset the moonroof control unit, do these steps:

1. Close the driver's door, and leave it closed until the procedure is complete.

2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

3. Press and hold the tilt switch, and turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

4. Release the tilt switch, and turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

5. Repeat steps 3 and 4 four more times.

6. Check if the AUTO OPEN and AUTO CLOSE function still work. If they still work, the AUTO functions have not been cleared, go back to step 1. If the AUTO functions have been cleared, go to step 7.

7. Press and hold the moonroof close switch until the moonroof is tilted fully open.

8. Confirm that the moonroof control unit is reset by using the moonroof AUTO OPEN and AUTO CLOSE function.
````

## Chunk 3624: Moonroof Motor-Control Unit Input Test (2/4-door)

- Title: Moonroof Motor-Control Unit Input Test (2/4-door)
- Source path: `pages\3217.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_225407f50259`

````text
# Moonroof Motor-Control Unit Input Test (2/4-door)

NOTE:

- Before testing, make sure the No. B19 fuse is OK.

- If the moonroof works OK manually, but will not work in AUTO, or reverses frequently (obstacle detection), reset the moonroof motor-control unit before proceeding with the input test.

1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

2. Disconnect the moonroof motor-control unit 14P connector .

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Inspect the connector and socket terminals to be sure they are all making good contact:

- If the terminals are bent, loose, or corroded, repair them as necessary, and recheck the system.

- If the terminals are OK, go to step 4.

With the connectors still disconnected, do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, go to step 5.

Cavity | Wire | Test condition | Test: Desired result | Possible cause if desired result is not obtained

12 *1 | WHT | Disconnect these connectors: Power window master switch 37P connector Front passenger's power window switch 37P connector | Check for continuity between terminal No. 12 and power window master switch 37P connector terminal No. 36: There should be continuity. | An open in the LIN(P/W) wire

- Power window master switch 37P connector

- Front passenger's power window switch 37P connector

Check for continuity between terminal No. 12 and front passenger's power window switch 37P connector terminal No. 34: There should be continuity. | An open in the LIN(P/W) wire

Check for continuity to ground: There should be no continuity. | A short to ground in the LIN(P/W) wire

*1: 4-door

*2: 2-door

Cavity | Wire | Test condition | Test: Desired result | Possible cause if desired result is not obtained

12 *2 | WHT | Disconnect these connectors: Power window master switch 37P connector Passenger's power window switch 37P connector | Check for continuity between terminal No. 12 and power window master switch 37P connector terminal No. 36: There should be continuity. | An open in the LIN(P/W) wire

- Power window master switch 37P connector

- Passenger's power window switch 37P connector

Check for continuity between terminal No. 12 and passenger's power window switch 37P connector terminal No. 34: There should be continuity. | An open in the LIN(P/W) wire

Check for continuity to ground: There should be no continuity. | A short to ground in the LIN(P/W) wire

*1: 4-door

*2: 2-door

Reconnect the connector, and do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, replace the moonroof motor-control unit .

Cavity | Wire | Test condition | Test: Desired result | Possible cause if desired result is not obtained

1 | GRN | Under all conditions | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be battery voltage. | Blown No. B19 fuse An open or high resistance in the wire

- Blown No. B19 fuse

- An open or high resistance in the wire

4 | BLK | In all power modes | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be less than 0.2 V. | Poor ground (G501 *1, G502 *2) An open or high resistance in the ground wire

- Poor ground (G501 *1, G502 *2)

- An open or high resistance in the ground wire

9 | LT GRN | Vehicle ON mode, moonroof switch in CLOSE position | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be less than 0.2 V. | Faulty moonroof switch Poor ground (G502) or an open in the ground wire An open or high resistance in the wire

- Faulty moonroof switch

- Poor ground (G502) or an open in the ground wire

- An open or high resistance in the wire

Vehicle ON mode, moonroof switch released | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be battery voltage. | Faulty moonroof switch A short to ground in the wire

- Faulty moonroof switch

- A short to ground in the wire

8 | PNK | Vehicle ON mode, moonroof switch in TILT position | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be less than 0.2 V. | Faulty moonroof switch Poor ground (G502) or an open in the ground wire An open or high resistance in the wire

- Faulty moonroof switch

- Poor ground (G502) or an open in the ground wire

- An open or high resistance in the wire

Vehicle ON mode, moonroof switch released | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be battery voltage. | Faulty moonroof switch A short to ground in the wire

- Faulty moonroof switch

- A short to ground in the wire
````

## Chunk 3629: Moonroof System Description - Control/Function

- Title: Moonroof System Description - Control/Function
- Source path: `pages\3220.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_ba2999716c21`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16143.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Moonroof System Description - Control/Function

Operation (Manual)

The moonroof is activated by pushing the switch to the first detent of the open or close position.

Operation (Auto)

The automatic operation of moonroof is activated by pushing the switch to the second detent of the open or close position. The moonroof will automatically open or close completely even if the switch is released.

Tilt function

Auto tilt up operation

During moonroof auto tilt operation, the moonroof will go to full tilt up position when the moonroof tilt switch is pushed and released.

Auto tilt down operation

The moonroof is moved to the full closing position when the close switch is pushed to second detent position, and then released.

Manual operation (only for tilt down)

In a tilted position, the moonroof is controlled to operate the tilt down when the close switch is pushed to first detent position.

Auto Reverse Function

When the moonroof is closing and the stopping load is greater than the prescribed value, the moonroof will move in reverse direction momentarily then stop. The control unit detects "Pinching" by checking the pulses output from the pulse sensor in the moonroof motor.

Learning Function

The learning function allows the moonroof to open and close with auto operation. If learning is not completed, the auto operation will not function.

Keyless/Key Cylinder Operation

By pressing and holding the UNLOCK button of the keyless transmitter or the remote a second time, the windows and moonroof open. The windows stop moving when the UNLOCK button is released.

With the key inserted in the driver's door key cylinder, turn the key a second time holding it (counterclockwise to close). The windows and moonroof stop moving when the key is released.

Keyless Operation | Open | Possible

Close | Impossible

Key Cylinder Operation | Open | Possible

Close | Possible
````

# VSA related DTCs (VSA Control Unit): Notes

NOTE:

- Initial Diagnosis: The initial on-board diagnosis occurs right after the vehicle condition is turned to the ON mode, and the ABS/VSA indicator comes on, and continues until the indicator goes off.

The initial on-board diagnosis occurs right after the vehicle condition is turned to the ON mode, and the ABS/VSA indicator comes on, and continues until the indicator goes off.

- Regular Diagnosis: The regular on-board diagnosis begins right after the ABS/VSA indicator goes off, and it continues until the vehicle condition is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

The regular on-board diagnosis begins right after the ABS/VSA indicator goes off, and it continues until the vehicle condition is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

- Review "How to troubleshoot the VSA system" before doing the DTC troubleshooting procedure.

- Test-drive the vehicle according to local traffic conditions and regulations. Do not test-drive on a lift unless specified.

- The possible causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.
````

# DTC C0023-12: Brake Diag Circuit ON Failure

Confirmation Procedure | Basic Condition: 12 volt battery voltage 10 V or more Operating Condition: Turn the vehicle to the ON mode and press the brake pedal for at least 2 seconds.

- 12 volt battery voltage 10 V or more

Operating Condition:

- Turn the vehicle to the ON mode and press the brake pedal for at least 2 seconds.

Execution | Regular Diagnosis

Duration | About 2 seconds

DTC Type | 1 drive cycle Brake system indicator (amber) on

- 1 drive cycle

- Brake system indicator (amber) on

Fail Safe Action | Stop automatic brake hold function Stop driving support system's brake function Stop emergency brake function

- Stop automatic brake hold function

- Stop driving support system's brake function

- Stop emergency brake function

Possible Cause | Short to ground between the VSA modulator-control unit and the relay circuit board (built-in brake light relay) (BRK LAMP line) Open or Poor connection between the PCM and the brake pedal position switch (STOP SW line) Relay circuit board (built-in brake light relay) failure PCM internal failure VSA modulator-control unit internal failure

- Short to ground between the VSA modulator-control unit and the relay circuit board (built-in brake light relay) (BRK LAMP line)

- Open or Poor connection between the PCM and the brake pedal position switch (STOP SW line)

- Relay circuit board (built-in brake light relay) failure

- PCM internal failure

- VSA modulator-control unit internal failure
````

## Chunk 3730: System Indicator

- Title: System Indicator
- Source path: `pages\3379.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_878f3fe39921`
- Images: `images\GHH412813.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3740.html`, `pages\25127.html`, `pages\16792.html`

### Full Text

````text
# System Indicator

This system has seven indicators on the gauge control module.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

NOTE: For locations of each indicator, refer to gauge control module component location index .

When the system is OK, each indicator comes on for about 2 seconds after turning the vehicle to the ON mode, then it goes off.

When the system detects a problem, a DTC will set and depending upon the failure, the VSA modulator-control unit determines which indicator(s) will turn on. If the problem goes away (system returns to normal), the indicator(s) will be controlled in the following way depending upon the DTC that was set:

- The indicator(s) will come on and stay on when turning the vehicle to the ON mode.

- The indicator(s) will automatically go off.

- The indicator(s) will go off after the vehicle is driven.
````

# Brake System Indicator (Red)

The brake system indicator (red) comes on when the EBD function is lost, the parking brake is applied, and/or the brake fluid level is low.

NOTE: If two or more wheel speed sensors fail, the brake system indicator (red) will come on.
````

## Chunk 3738: Diagnostic Trouble Code (DTC)

- Title: Diagnostic Trouble Code (DTC)
- Source path: `pages\3387.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_175a0a233909`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3748.html`, `pages\25135.html`, `pages\16800.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Diagnostic Trouble Code (DTC)

- The memory can hold all DTCs. However, when the same DTC is detected more than once, the more recent DTC is written over the earlier one. Therefore, when the same problem is detected repeatedly, it is memorized as a single DTC.

- The DTCs are memorized in an EEPROM in the VSA modulator-control unit. Therefore, the memorized DTCs cannot be erased by disconnecting the 12 volt battery. Do the specified procedures to clear the DTCs.
````

## Chunk 3739: How to Troubleshoot the VSA System: Self-Diagnosis

- Title: How to Troubleshoot the VSA System: Self-Diagnosis

# How to Troubleshoot the VSA System: Self-Diagnosis

- Self-diagnosis can be classified into two categories:

- - Initial diagnosis: Done right after turning the vehicle to the ON mode and until the ABS, VSA, and brake system indicator (amber) go off.

- - Initial diagnosis: Done right after turning the vehicle to the ON mode and until the ABS, VSA, and brake system indicator (amber) go off.

Initial diagnosis: Done right after turning the vehicle to the ON mode and until the ABS, VSA, and brake system indicator (amber) go off.

- - Regular diagnosis: Done right after the initial diagnosis until turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

- - Regular diagnosis: Done right after the initial diagnosis until turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

Regular diagnosis: Done right after the initial diagnosis until turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

- When the system detects a problem, the VSA modulator-control unit shifts to fail-safe mode.
````

## Chunk 3743: How to Troubleshoot DTCs

- Title: How to Troubleshoot DTCs
- Source path: `pages\3392.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_f769d7c48c9c`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3753.html`, `pages\25140.html`, `pages\16805.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Troubleshoot DTCs

The troubleshooting procedures assume that the cause of the problem is still present and the ABS, VSA, and/or brake system indicator (amber) are still on. Following a troubleshooting procedure for a DTC that has been cleared but does not reset can result in incorrect diagnosis.

1. Question the customer about the conditions when the problem occurred, and try to reproduce the same conditions for troubleshooting. Find out when the ABS, VSA, and/or brake system indicator (amber) came on, such as during activation, after activation, when the vehicle was traveling at a certain speed, etc. If necessary, have the customer demonstrate the concern.

2. When the ABS, VSA, or brake system indicator (amber) does not come on during the test-drive, but troubleshooting is done based on the DTC, check for loose connectors, poor contact of the terminals, etc. in the circuit indicated by the DTC.

3. After troubleshooting, or the repairs are done, clear the DTCs, and test-drive the vehicle under the same conditions that originally set the DTCs. Make sure the ABS, VSA and brake system indicator (amber) do not come on.
````

## Chunk 3744: How to Use the HDS (Honda Diagnostic System)

- Title: How to Use the HDS (Honda Diagnostic System)
- Source path: `pages\3394.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_cae4a5da2ee6`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3755.html`, `pages\25142.html`, `pages\16807.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Use the HDS (Honda Diagnostic System)

NOTE: Make sure the 12 volt battery is in good condition and fully charged.

1. If the system indicators stay on, connect the HDS to the data link connector (DLC) .

2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

3. Make sure the HDS communicates with the vehicle and the VSA modulator-control unit. If it does not, go to the DLC circuit troubleshooting .

4. Check the diagnostic trouble code (DTC) for all systems, troubleshoot the powertrain DTCs first. Then refer to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting, and do the appropriate troubleshooting procedure.

NOTE:

- Freeze data indicates the VSA conditions when the first system malfunction that activated the indicator was detected.

- The HDS can read the DTC, the freeze data, the current data, and other system data.

- For specific operations, refer to the HDS Help menu.
````

## Chunk 3745: How to Retrieve DTCs

- Title: How to Retrieve DTCs
- Source path: `pages\3395.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_1f6d42dd9ea1`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3756.html`, `pages\25143.html`, `pages\16808.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Retrieve DTCs

1. With the vehicle in OFF (LOCK) mode, connect the HDS to the data link connector (DLC)

2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

3. Make sure the HDS communicates with the vehicle and the VSA modulator-control unit. If it does not, go to the DLC circuit troubleshooting

4. Follow the prompts on the HDS to display the DTC(s) on the screen. After determining the DTC, refer to the DTC troubleshooting. Do the all systems DTC check, and troubleshoot any powertrain DTCs first.

5. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.
````

## Chunk 3746: How to Clear DTCs

- Title: How to Clear DTCs
- Source path: `pages\3396.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_dbe9dd3813ff`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3757.html`, `pages\25144.html`, `pages\16809.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Clear DTCs

1. With the vehicle in OFF (LOCK) mode, connect the HDS to the data link connector (DLC)

2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

3. Make sure the HDS communicates with the vehicle and the VSA modulator-control unit. If it does not, go to the DLC circuit troubleshooting

4. Clear the DTC(s) by following the screen prompts on the HDS.

5. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.
````

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode. -4. Check for DTCs with the HDS. # DTC C0023-14 (5-door) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC C0023-14 : Brake Diag Circuit OFF Failure

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C0023-14 Brake Diag Circuit OFF Failure

DTCs (VSA)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode. -4. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame C0023-14 Brake Diag Circuit OFF Failure Is DTC C0023-14 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode.

-4. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

C0023-14 Brake Diag Circuit OFF Failure

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

- Fuse check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Check the following fuse. Fuse No. A8 (10 A) Location Under-hood fuse/relay box Is the fuse OK? YES The fuse is OK. Reinstall the fuse, then go to step 3. NO Replace the fuse. If the fuse blows again, repair the short to body ground on the No. A8 (10 A) fuse circuit.

NO

Connect terminals 1 and 2 with a jumper wire. Terminal 1 VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector No. 34 Terminal 2 Body ground -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Check the brake lights without pressing the brake pedal. Are the brake lights on? YES Go to step 7. NO Go to step 4.

YES

Go to step 7.

NO

# DTC C0030-38, C0033-38, C0036-38, C0039-38

DTC C0030-38 : Magnetic Encoder (Left Front) Failure (Pulse Missing)

DTC C0033-38 : Magnetic Encoder (Right Front) Failure (Pulse Missing)

DTC C0036-38 : Magnetic Encoder (Left Rear) Failure (Pulse Missing)

DTC C0039-38 : Magnetic Encoder (Right Rear) Failure (Pulse Missing)

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C0030-38 Magnetic Encoder (Left Front) Failure (Pulse Missing)

C0033-38 Magnetic Encoder (Right Front) Failure (Pulse Missing)

C0036-38 Magnetic Encoder (Left Rear) Failure (Pulse Missing)

C0039-38 Magnetic Encoder (Right Rear) Failure (Pulse Missing)

DTCs (VSA)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Test-drive the vehicle on the road in a straight line over 19 mph (30km/h) for 30 seconds or more. NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame C0030-38 Magnetic Encoder (Left Front) Failure (Pulse Missing) C0033-38 Magnetic Encoder (Right Front) Failure (Pulse Missing) DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame C0036-38 Magnetic Encoder (Left Rear) Failure (Pulse Missing) C0039-38 Magnetic Encoder (Right Rear) Failure (Pulse Missing) Is DTC C0030-38, C0033-38, C0036-38, or C0039-38 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Test-drive the vehicle on the road in a straight line over 19 mph (30km/h) for 30 seconds or more.

NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift.

-5. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C0030-38 Magnetic Encoder (Left Front) Failure (Pulse Missing)

C0033-38 Magnetic Encoder (Right Front) Failure (Pulse Missing)

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C0036-38 Magnetic Encoder (Left Rear) Failure (Pulse Missing)

C0039-38 Magnetic Encoder (Right Rear) Failure (Pulse Missing)

Is DTC C0030-38, C0033-38, C0036-38, or C0039-38 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot.

- Magnetic encoder check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Inspect the appropriate magnetic encoder for damage, debris, and correct installation. DTC Appropriate Magnetic Encoder Note C0030-38 Left-front Remove the driveshaft outboard joint from the appropriate wheel hub . C0033-38 Right-front C0036-38 Left-rear Remove the hub bearing unit . C0039-38 Right-rear Is the magnetic encoder surface OK? YES Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the VSA modulator-control unit . NO Clean the debris from the appropriate magnetic encoder surface on the wheel bearing or the hub bearing unit. If the DTC is still present, replace the appropriate wheel bearing or the hub bearing unit .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Inspect the appropriate magnetic encoder for damage, debris, and correct installation.

DTC | Appropriate Magnetic Encoder | Note

C0030-38 | Left-front | Remove the driveshaft outboard joint from the appropriate wheel hub .

C0033-38 | Right-front

C0036-38 | Left-rear | Remove the hub bearing unit .

C0039-38 | Right-rear

Is the magnetic encoder surface OK?

YES

Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

NO

Clean the debris from the appropriate magnetic encoder surface on the wheel bearing or the hub bearing unit. If the DTC is still present, replace the appropriate wheel bearing or the hub bearing unit .
````

# DTC C0031-14, C0034-14, C0037-14, C003A-14 (With Adaptive Damper System) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC C0031-14 : Left Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

DTC C0034-14 : Right Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

DTC C0037-14 : Left Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

# DTC C0031-14, C0034-14, C0037-14, C003A-14 (Without Adaptive Damper System)

DTC C0031-14 : Left Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

DTC C0034-14 : Right Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

DTC C0037-14 : Left Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C0031-14 Left Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

C0034-14 Right Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

C0037-14 Left Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

C003A-14 Right Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open)

DTCs (VSA)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Test-drive the vehicle on the road in a straight line at 19 mph (30km/h) or more. NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame C0031-14 Left Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open) C0034-14 Right Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open) C0037-14 Left Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open) C003A-14 Right Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open) Is DTC C0031-14, C0034-14, C0037-14, or C003A-14 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Test-drive the vehicle on the road in a straight line at 19 mph (30km/h) or more.

NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift.

# DTC C0031-62, C0034-62, C0037-62, C003A-62 (VSA Control Unit)

DTC C0031-62 : Left Front Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Compare Failure

DTC C0034-62 : Right Front Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Compare Failure

DTC C0037-62 : Left Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Compare Failure

DTC C003A-62 : Right Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Compare Failure

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

C0037-62 Left Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Compare Failure

DTCs (VSA)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Test-drive the vehicle on the road in a straight line over 19 mph (30km/h) for 60 seconds or more. NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot.

# DTC C1100-98

DTC C1100-98 : Electric Parking Brake Continuous Operations Detected

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- This DTC is set when repeated use of the electric parking brake function causes electric parking brake actuator overheating. The code can be cleared with the HDS once the electric parking brake actuator has cooled.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C1100-98 Electric Parking Brake Continuous Operations Detected

YES Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot.

-6. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC C1120-96 : Electric Parking Brake Switch Malfunction

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- This DTC is detected when operating the electric parking brake switch continuously for more than 20 seconds.

- Check that electric parking brake switch is free of dirt and other foreign particles.

YES

NO

## Chunk 3824: DTC C1C22-62

### Full Text

````text
# DTC C1C22-62

DTC C1C22-62 : Neutral Sensor Failure

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C1C22-62 Neutral Sensor Failure

DTCs (VSA)

- DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Test-drive the vehicle at 19 mph (30 km/h) or more for 60 seconds or more. NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame U0029-00 CAN Communication Failure (Bus OFF) U0101-00 CAN Communication Failure with TCM C1C22-62 Neutral Sensor Failure Is DTC C1C22-62 indicated? YES If DTC C1C22-62 is only indicated, go to step 2. YES If DTC U0029-00 or U0101-00 are also indicated, go to U0029-00 or U0101-00 . NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Test-drive the vehicle at 19 mph (30 km/h) or more for 60 seconds or more.

NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift.

-5. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

U0029-00 CAN Communication Failure (Bus OFF)

U0101-00 CAN Communication Failure with TCM

C1C22-62 Neutral Sensor Failure

Is DTC C1C22-62 indicated?

YES

If DTC C1C22-62 is only indicated, go to step 2.

YES

If DTC U0029-00 or U0101-00 are also indicated, go to U0029-00 or U0101-00 .

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot.

- CVT system parameter check -1. Check the parameter(s) below with the HDS. Signal Current conditions Values Unit A/T N Switch Does the parameter changes when the shift lever is in neutral, and then shift lever is not in neutral? YES Go to step 3. NO Check the transmission range switch .

-1. Check the parameter(s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

A/T N Switch

Does the parameter changes when the shift lever is in neutral, and then shift lever is not in neutral?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Check the transmission range switch .

- VSA modulator-control unit check -1. Check the parameter(s) below with the HDS. Signal Current conditions Values Unit NEUTRAL SWITCH STATUS Does the parameter changes when the shift lever is in neutral, and then shift lever is not in neutral? YES The system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting. NO Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

# DTC U0029-00 (VSA Control Unit: With ACC)

DTC U0029-00 : CAN Communication Failure (Bus OFF)

NOTE:

- This DTC is stored by communication failure between the F-CAN line and the VSA modulator-control unit. When DTC U0029-00 is indicated, the F-CAN line has already returned to normal. Check for loose terminals and poor connections at the connectors. If the connectors are OK, clear the DTC and follow this troubleshooting.

- Troubleshoot any PGM-FI system DTCs first .

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | DTC

U0029-00 CAN Communication Failure (Bus OFF)

DTC (VSA)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then wait for a few seconds or more. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC U0029-00 CAN Communication Failure (Bus OFF) Is U0029-00 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then wait for a few seconds or more.

-5. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

U0029-00 CAN Communication Failure (Bus OFF)

Is U0029-00 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting .

- CAN gateway system DTC check -1. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Are any DTCs indicated? YES Go to indicated DTC's troubleshooting. NO Go to step 3.

-1. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

Are any DTCs indicated?

YES

Go to indicated DTC's troubleshooting.

NO

Go to step 3.

- F-CAN circuit communication check -1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check. F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check Does the CAN gateway detect the VSA modulator-control unit Bus channels A and B? '16-18 2/4-door: VSA modulator-control unit is Not Available for Bus A Go to step 4. '16-18 2/4-door: VSA modulator-control unit is Not Available for Bus B Go to step 5. '19-21 2/4-door: VSA modulator-control unit is Not Available for Bus A Go to step 6. '19-21 2/4-door: VSA modulator-control unit is Not Available for Bus B Go to step 7. '17-19 5-door: VSA modulator-control unit is Not Available for Bus A Go to step 4. '17-19 5-door: VSA modulator-control unit is Not Available for Bus B Go to step 5. '20-21 5-door: VSA modulator-control unit is Not Available for Bus A Go to step 6. '20-21 5-door: VSA modulator-control unit is Not Available for Bus B Go to step 7. VSA modulator-control unit Bus A and B are Available Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the VSA modulator-control unit connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting or replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

## Chunk 3829: DTC U0029-00 (VSA Control Unit: Without ACC)

- Title: DTC U0029-00 (VSA Control Unit: Without ACC)
- Source path: `pages\3445.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_dd3c3cfa3969`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3806.html`, `pages\25193.html`, `pages\16776.html`

### Full Text

````text
# DTC U0029-00 (VSA Control Unit: Without ACC)

DTC U0029-00 : CAN Communication Failure (Bus OFF)

NOTE:

- This DTC is stored by communication failure between the F-CAN line and the VSA modulator-control unit. When DTC U0029-00 is indicated, the F-CAN line has already returned to normal. Check for loose terminals and poor connections at the connectors. If the connectors are OK, clear the DTC and follow this troubleshooting.

- Troubleshoot any PGM-FI system DTCs first .

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

U0029-00 CAN Communication Failure (Bus OFF)

DTCs (VSA)

- Connector check (VSA modulator-control unit connector) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Check for loose terminals and poor connections at the following connector. VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector Is the connector OK? YES Go to step 2. NO Repair loose terminal or poor connection. Then clear the DTC by using the HDS and recheck DTC U0029-00. If DTC U0029-00 is not confirmed, troubleshooting is complete.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Check for loose terminals and poor connections at the following connector.

VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector

Is the connector OK?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Repair loose terminal or poor connection. Then clear the DTC by using the HDS and recheck DTC U0029-00. If DTC U0029-00 is not confirmed, troubleshooting is complete.

- VSA modulator-control unit communication check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then start the engine. -2. Check for communication between the VSA modulator-control unit and the HDS. Is there communication between the VSA modulator-control unit and the HDS? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot. NO Go to DLC circuit troubleshooting .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then start the engine.

-2. Check for communication between the VSA modulator-control unit and the HDS.

Is there communication between the VSA modulator-control unit and the HDS?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the Freeze data/on-board snapshot.

NO

Go to DLC circuit troubleshooting .
````

# DTC U0100-00, U0101-00, U0104-00, U0123-00, U0126-00, U0151-00, U0155-00 (2/4-door (With ACC)) (2019 2020 2021)

DTC U0100-00 : CAN Communication Failure with ECM/ISU

DTC U0101-00 : CAN Communication Failure with TCM

DTC U0104-00 : CAN Communication Failure with ACC

DTC U0123-00 : CAN Communication Failure with Yaw-Rate/Acceleration Sensor

DTC U0126-00 : CAN Communication Failure with Absolute Steering Wheel Angle Sensor

DTC U0151-00 : CAN Communication Failure with SRS

DTC U0155-00 : CAN Communication Failure with Meter

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Refer to the DTC code shown on the display, and then inspect the connectors and terminals based on the instructions in the table.

- These DTC(s) may be stored due to a power/ground circuit failure of the appropriate transmitting control unit. Before doing this troubleshooting, check the power/ground circuit of the appropriate transmitting control unit first.

DTC Description | DTC

U0100-00 CAN Communication Failure with ECM/ISU

U0101-00 CAN Communication Failure with TCM

U0104-00 CAN Communication Failure with ACC

U0123-00 CAN Communication Failure with Yaw-Rate/Acceleration Sensor

U0126-00 CAN Communication Failure with Absolute Steering Wheel Angle Sensor

U0151-00 CAN Communication Failure with SRS

U0155-00 CAN Communication Failure with Meter

DTCs (VSA)

- CAN gateway system DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Are there DTCs indicated? YES Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) . NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

Are there DTCs indicated?

YES

Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) .

NO

Go to step 2.

- F-CAN circuit communication check (Receiving control unit) -1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check. F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check Does the CAN gateway detect the VSA modulator-control unit Bus channels A and B? Bus A is Not Available Go to step 3. Bus B is Not Available Go to step 4. Bus A and B are Available Go to step 5.

-1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check.

F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check

Does the CAN gateway detect the VSA modulator-control unit Bus channels A and B?

Bus A is Not Available

Go to step 3.

Bus B is Not Available

Go to step 4.

Bus A and B are Available

Go to step 5.

- Open wire check (F-CAN A_H line, F-CAN A_L line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connectors. CAN gateway 16P connector VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector -3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode CAN gateway 16P connector: disconnected VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector: disconnected Test point 1 CAN gateway 16P connector (female terminals) No. 3: Test point 2 VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector No. 20 Test point 1 CAN gateway 16P connector (female terminals) No. 11: Test point 2 VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector No. 21 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES The F-CAN A_H and F-CAN A_L wires are OK. Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the VSA modulator-control unit connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting or replace the VSA modulator-control unit . NO Repair an open in the F-CAN A_H wire or the F-CAN A_L wire between the VSA modulator-control unit and the CAN gateway.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the following connectors.

CAN gateway 16P connector

VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector

-3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.

CAN gateway 16P connector: disconnected

Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode CAN gateway 16P connector: disconnected Transmitting control unit connector(s) for indicated DTCs: disconnected DTC Circuit name Test point 1 (Receiving control unit) Test point 2 (Transmitting control unit) Connector Terminal Connector Terminal U0100-00 F-CAN A_H CAN gateway 16P connector (female terminals) : No. 3 PCM connector A (50P) No. 37 F-CAN A_L No. 11 No. 36 U0101-00 F-CAN A_H CAN gateway 16P connector (female terminals) : No. 3 TCM 50P connector No. 3 F-CAN A_L No. 11 No. 11 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. DTC Operation for transmitting control unit U0100-00 Substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If DTC U0100-00 goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM . U0101-00 Substitute a known-good TCM , then recheck. If DTC U0101-00 goes away and the TCM was substituted, replace the original TCM . -3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.

# DTC U0100-00, U0101-00, U0104-00, U0123-00, U0126-00, U0151-00, U0155-00 (5-door (With ACC)) (2019)

DTC U0100-00 : CAN Communication Failure with ECM/ISU

DTC U0101-00 : CAN Communication Failure with TCM

DTC U0104-00 : CAN Communication Failure with ACC

DTC U0123-00 : CAN Communication Failure with Yaw-Rate/Acceleration Sensor

DTC U0126-00 : CAN Communication Failure with Absolute Steering Wheel Angle Sensor

DTC U0151-00 : CAN Communication Failure with SRS

DTC U0155-00 : CAN Communication Failure with Meter

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Refer to the DTC code shown on the display, and then inspect the connectors and terminals based on the instructions in the table.

- These DTC(s) may be stored due to a power/ground circuit failure of the appropriate transmitting control unit. Before doing this troubleshooting, check the power/ground circuit of the appropriate transmitting control unit first.

DTC Description | DTC

U0100-00 CAN Communication Failure with ECM/ISU

U0101-00 CAN Communication Failure with TCM

U0104-00 CAN Communication Failure with ACC

U0123-00 CAN Communication Failure with Yaw-Rate/Acceleration Sensor

U0126-00 CAN Communication Failure with Absolute Steering Wheel Angle Sensor

U0151-00 CAN Communication Failure with SRS

U0155-00 CAN Communication Failure with Meter

DTC (VSA)

- CAN gateway system DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Are there DTCs indicated? YES Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) . NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS.

YES

Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) .

NO

Go to step 2.

- F-CAN circuit communication check (Receiving control unit) -1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check. F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check Does the CAN gateway detect the VSA modulator-control unit Bus channels A and B? Bus A is Not Available Go to step 3. Bus B is Not Available Go to step 4. Bus A and B are Available Go to step 5.

-1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check.

F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check

Does the CAN gateway detect the VSA modulator-control unit Bus channels A and B?

Bus A is Not Available

Go to step 3.

Bus B is Not Available

Go to step 4.

Bus A and B are Available

Go to step 5.

- Open wire check (F-CAN A_H line, F-CAN A_L line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connectors. CAN gateway 12P connector VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector -3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode CAN gateway 12P connector: disconnected VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector: disconnected Test point 1 CAN gateway 12P connector (female terminals) No. 3: Test point 2 VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector No. 20 Test point 1 CAN gateway 12P connector (female terminals) No. 9: Test point 2 VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector No. 21 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there 1.0 Ω or less ? YES The F-CAN A_H and F-CAN A_L wires are OK. Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the VSA modulator-control unit connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting or replace the VSA modulator-control unit . NO Repair an open in the F-CAN A_H wire or the F-CAN A_L wire between the VSA modulator-control unit and the CAN gateway.

# DTC U0100-00, U0101-00, U0104-00, U0123-00, U0126-00, U0151-00, U0155-00 (5-door (With ACC)) (2020 2021)

DTC U0100-00 : CAN Communication Failure with ECM/ISU

DTC U0101-00 : CAN Communication Failure with TCM

DTC U0104-00 : CAN Communication Failure with ACC

DTC U0123-00 : CAN Communication Failure with Yaw-Rate/Acceleration Sensor

DTC U0126-00 : CAN Communication Failure with Absolute Steering Wheel Angle Sensor

DTC U0151-00 : CAN Communication Failure with SRS

DTC U0155-00 : CAN Communication Failure with Meter

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Refer to the DTC code shown on the display, and then inspect the connectors and terminals based on the instructions in the table.

- These DTC(s) may be stored due to a power/ground circuit failure of the appropriate transmitting control unit. Before doing this troubleshooting, check the power/ground circuit of the appropriate transmitting control unit first.

DTC Description | DTC

U0100-00 CAN Communication Failure with ECM/ISU

U0101-00 CAN Communication Failure with TCM

U0104-00 CAN Communication Failure with ACC

U0123-00 CAN Communication Failure with Yaw-Rate/Acceleration Sensor

U0126-00 CAN Communication Failure with Absolute Steering Wheel Angle Sensor

U0151-00 CAN Communication Failure with SRS

U0155-00 CAN Communication Failure with Meter

DTC (VSA)

- CAN gateway system DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Are there DTCs indicated? YES Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) . NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

Are there DTCs indicated?

YES

Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) .

NO

Go to step 2.

- F-CAN circuit communication check (Receiving control unit) -1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check. F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check Does the CAN gateway detect the VSA modulator-control unit Bus channels A and B? Bus A is Not Available Go to step 3. Bus B is Not Available Go to step 4. Bus A and B are Available Go to step 5.

-1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check.

F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check

Does the CAN gateway detect the VSA modulator-control unit Bus channels A and B?

Bus A is Not Available

Go to step 3.

Bus B is Not Available

Go to step 4.

Bus A and B are Available

Go to step 5.

- Open wire check (F-CAN A_H line, F-CAN A_L line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connectors. CAN gateway 16P connector VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector -3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode CAN gateway 16P connector: disconnected VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector: disconnected Test point 1 CAN gateway 16P connector (female terminals) No. 3: Test point 2 VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector No. 20 Test point 1 CAN gateway 16P connector (female terminals) No. 11: Test point 2 VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector No. 21 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there 1.0 Ω or less ? YES The F-CAN A_H and F-CAN A_L wires are OK. Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the VSA modulator-control unit connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting or replace the VSA modulator-control unit . NO Repair an open in the F-CAN A_H wire or the F-CAN A_L wire between the VSA modulator-control unit and the CAN gateway.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the following connectors.

CAN gateway 16P connector

VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector

-3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.
